Point Report by the Committee on Justice on the motion for a resolution (Doc.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-04-28-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00126_d8e5181	We now come to points 1 to 3 of the agenda on which the debate is being held under one. The first speech comes from Mr Hübner. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-04-28-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00126_d8e5205	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! All three items on the agenda are essentially about the demarcation between the victim's rights and the rights of the accused. This is a very delicate issue, a subject on which the criminal justice has been dealing for many thousand years, one can say. We assume – at least in the European tradition – that we rather – “more than say” – take into account the fact that a debtor goes free than an innocent person is condemned. So we're not as radical as the Old Testament says: If you'd rather live a thousand guilty people than destroy an innocent man. – That's not how we see it, but a little bit is going in this direction. All that is on the agenda today does not invariably shift the weight in favour of victim protection and the accused – in small doses, but in problematic doses. Nevertheless, we in principle agree with the projects, but believe that there is some need for improvement and that without these improvements in the decision, it should not be taken. This concerns, for example, the understanding of accounts. We have now provided that the prosecutor can also view the accounts register without judicial authorisation, a collection of all accounts of a suspect person. This is, of course, a very, very serious intervention into privacy and the rights of a person solely due to suspect status. We believe that it is essential to link this to at least a judicial authorisation. We have therefore tabled an amendment, namely by Mr Hübner and other Members, who, I am now not going to explain it legislatively, states that in paragraph 28 of § 116 para. 3 of the Criminal Procedure Order is chosen as follows: ‘Information from the account register shall be ordered by the public prosecutor on the basis of a judicial authorisation. § 116 para. 4 shall apply mutatis mutandis.’ In addition, “in paragraph 28 (...) in § 116 para. 5 (...) after the word ‘account register’ the word sequence ‘including judicial authorisation’ is inserted. That is to say, you have to add this judicial authorisation to the reporting account register, only then you get information. I call for the approval of this amendment. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] We will refuse to accept the amendments we have tabled, and I will justify this as follows: There is once again Mr Steinacker's request, Mr Jarolim, which essentially aims to ensure that, contrary to the findings of the Constitutional Court, the images and recordings of the so-called counter-dictorial hearing in the preliminary proceedings are no longer to be followed by the defenders. [[Abg. Gisela Worm: Yes, opinion of Foresthuber!]] – Yes, think Foresthuber and some others. But I think that the Constitutional Court is right: Especially in the counter-dictorial hearing, where it is true that the victim no longer needs to appear at the main trial – an essential proof chapter is done in the preliminary proceedings, often the most essential – it will, I think, be necessary to provide the defender with these tape tracks or film recordings. It's very important to see how he answers, he slows down, what kind of facial expression does he have, how long does he need to answer a question. All of this is not apparent from the copy protocol, which is now planned instead of handing out the recordings. That is why I believe that there is a significant restriction on rights here. Especially in the case of crimes against sexual integrity, it is often the only straining evidence that the victim says. There are, of course, a lot of misconceptions here, here very often on the question of voluntaryity/involuntaryness and so on, no objective evidence is tangible. It is similar with the two other applications aimed at strengthening the two categories of actions. There are acts that lead to particularly vulnerable victims, and others that do not lead to particularly vulnerable victims. The victims then have different types of rights. Of course, everything that is so-called hate crime leads to particularly vulnerable and particularly caring victims. All that does not meet such a criterion does not lead to particularly vulnerable victims. That is, if an old lady of pure greed falls into the park, is robbed and seriously injured, or if his life savings are removed by a skillful fraudulent system, then you are not a particularly vulnerable victim for a long distance. [[Abg. Steinhauser: Oh yes!]] - No, you're not! It may be, but you're not. The question is: Should we expand this? Should it be a decision by the judges to decide whether or not to grant a particular status of protection or not, or should this not be settled objectively? – I am rather skeptical of these two classes of victims, I think a victim is a victim, and therefore we will not agree with the requests for them. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-04-28-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00126_d8e5368	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! In order to ensure the effectiveness of the new provisions on the protection of victims, I make the following request: [[Quoted print version removed]] Ladies and gentlemen! In addition to improving the protection of victims, the 2016 Criminal Procedure Change Act also provides for the introduction of the investigation measure of information from the account register in the StPO. This will take a further step towards combating economic crime after a number of measures already taken. Facilitating access to external account data is intended to simplify and speed up procedures. Especially in economic criminal cases and in connection with the acquisition or granting of legal aid, procedures are often criticized as difficult and too long. But, ladies and gentlemen, unlike Mr Hübner, I believe that it is justified to have an order from the public prosecutor for information from the account register. Unlike the much more intervention-intensive information on bank accounts and bank transactions, for which a judicial authorisation is of course required, the public prosecutor acquires only the external account data through the insight into the account register. In the sense of proportionality, this intervention should be regarded as a less fundamental right intervention than the information on internal account data. This measure should therefore be welcomed as well as the improvement of the protection of victims. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-04-28-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00126_d8e5523	Ladies and gentlemen! Mr. Minister! The core of this law is the expansion of the protection of victims. It is to be predicted that the basic intensification of the novelle our consent I think that in Austria we can be proud of not only a functioning, but also very well-developed victim protection. Nevertheless, there are some points that go into detail, but which show that the protection of victims in Austria would still have to be improved. These are suggestions which come from victim protection institutions, which have been introduced to us – I am assuming, also to the Minister of Justice, – where we could achieve something or something with small additions. The first approach comes from a very important victim protection facility, the victim protection body dealing with trafficking in human beings. This is the LEFÖ victim protection facility. It has decreed the desire that the victims of exploitation should be explicitly called in the name of victims who have experienced victim protection. I know that the Ministry of Justice is of the opinion that victims of trafficking in human beings are in principle involved in the protection of victims and are also entitled to psychosocial and legal process support, and I also know that the Ministry of Justice is actually financing this, but, on the basis of details, cases where there is a gap and therefore very specific groups of victims affected by trafficking in human beings would not be included in the law. LEFÖ points out this. The second suggestion comes from the largest Austrian victim protection facility, from the white ring, which has a very high reputation. On the one hand, it regrets that, in the case of the particular need for protection, unlike the directive, those groups of victims who are to receive this particular need for protection are not explicitly referred to. Which groups of victims, for example, are they about? – It’s about victims of trafficking in human beings, victims of disability, victims of hate crime, victims of organised crime. It is already true in the argument of the Minister of Justice that this victim group can also be given the particular need for protection. The difference to the directive is only that these groups are explicitly mentioned there, giving a very concrete indication that the examination of the particular need for protection in these groups is important and that this group should be given this particular status of need for protection. I see the danger that, as is now defined and implemented in Austrian law, there is theoretically the possibility of this particular need for protection, but practically in reality this status is very rarely granted, because the reference to these special victim groups is missing. In addition, a group, namely the group of victims of hate crime, might have a much more difficult protection status for the characteristics currently defined, because it does not provide for personal characteristics to be taken into account in the examination of the particular need for protection. What do I mean? – If, for example, a Jewish citizen becomes the victim of an anti-Semitic attack, then of course the fact that he is Jewish faith is to take particular account and appreciate when assessing this abuse with regard to the particular need for protection of the victim. This feature is missing, and that could actually be an obstacle, because the law provides that the characteristics are taxatatively defined. There may be a need for a follow-up. Mr Hübner, we do not introduce two different levels of protection, but this is provided for in the law. This is linked to the fact that the law has known a level of protection so far and that the directive brings in a further level of protection. But that has nothing to do with our request. Another point, which is disputable and which has now been clearly recognized as worthy of discussion by the government parties, is the question: Who decides on the special victim status? The novel provides for the police to do this. The problem I see very specifically: The police are already overburdened, and now, as it were, they are supposed to take on another task, in particular, to carry out an examination of the characteristics such as the health and mental state of a victim and the circumstances of the act. I do believe that victim protection facilities would be the better place, even on the condition that there is no shortage of resources at all by the police. We are tabling an amendment to take these points into account. I also say: The government parties have indicated to us in the committee that they are still ready for change. We also sent the request to SPÖ and ÖVP, but there was no answer, which I actually regret, because usually at least a No comes – a yes would of course be more beautiful. Too bad, but it is. Mr Scherak of the NEOS has responded, and that is why this is now also a request tabled together with the NEOS: [[Quoted print version removed]] This is nothing but what I have now presented in legal terms. A sentence on the second point contained in this Act: We have introduced the account register. This law is now intended to ensure that justice is also given access. That's necessary. The architecture is such that no judicial approval is necessary for the insight into the account register. But, of course, if the bank transactions of a data subject are disclosed into the account, a judicial authorisation is necessary. That's also right and good that way. The only thing we see differently: It also has a possibility for the bank institution to object, and we think this gatekeeper function is wrong. That would be about like if the phone provider had a veto on a phone check. This gatekeeper function, first of all, is unwarranted, in my opinion, and only leads to an unpleasant loss of time for prosecutors who find out. In spite of these remarks, however, we will agree to the law because it goes in the right direction overall. - Thank you.

It is true that, in the sense of implementing the directive, we have also provided for provisions in the interest of those suspected of having committed criminal proceedings. It is intended to protect their confidential communication with their defender to a wider extent than before, and, in the sense of the MRP, illegal provocations of action are to be prevented by the threat of a procedural obstacle, specifically the termination of the procedure. These are the consequences of the European Convention on Human Rights, which is in our constitutional status. All in all, ladies and gentlemen, this is, as I believe, a balanced, reasonable package. It continues, if I may say so, the line of our house: the line of reason when it comes to precise and careful consideration of all the interests to be taken into account. I would like to comment briefly on some of the points that have been ventilated here in the course of the debate. As far as the account register is concerned, I would just like to make it clear once again that the information on the question of who has an account at which bank has already been given to the public prosecutors. This did not require any judicial authorisation so far, only so far that the prosecutors in principle had to write all the banks to find out whether or not a certain guilty person has an account connection. We now want to simplify this for the prosecutors through the central account register – that's all. So now that we are introducing additional obstacles or obstacles, it would not be useful or necessary and would, of course, also counter the purpose of the process acceleration, which is very important to us and which must be very important. On the subject of audiovisual recording as a model attempt, I would just like to say the following: Mr. Scherak, we have such model tests, they're already running. We have already experienced this. Those who work in practice know that also in the field of the economic and corruption prosecutorship, the revenues are usually recorded. It is already a considerable effort to make this really comprehensive and inconsistency, because it would have to start with the police, and then of course there are also questions about data protection and personality rights, as we have just seen today in relation to the records of counter-dictoral consents of victims of sexual offences. But we are working on it, and there are already experiences and initiatives to this end. One more thing: Of course, there is no question that rape, of course also attempted rape, constitutes a serious crime, which, according to current legal situation, legally allows for all consequences in the area of asylum and residence law. That's right now – I just wanted to have said that. In addition, I am pleased about the contributions to debate and the suggestions I have made to them, as well as the debate in the Committee on Justice, which was also very constructive. I say once again that we in the ministry have a really great and very effective team. Whenever there is the need for change, for further developments, we are happy to be there. Ladies and gentlemen: You wish we were playing! Today, as I believe, we have already implemented 35 laws in our area of competence, at least one per month. Finally, I would also like to thank you for your constructive support, and I hope that we will also be able to achieve some kind of improvement in this area. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-04-28-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00126_d8e6613	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Last year, criminal law was reformed, very complex and with great advance. After 40 years, we wanted to make a big criminal law reform, so all possible professionals were involved, and criminal law was reformed. . As a liberal, we did not vote because we had some criticisms, and two of these criticisms refer to today's agenda. One criticism was that the definition of commercialism was changed. – Commerciality was regulated in the old law in such a way that, when someone commits an act that obviously aims to secure himself continuously with this act of doing so, it was called commercial. This had a higher threat of punishment, and because of this provision, if one could prove this intention, one could arrest a perpetrator. Now we have changed this commerciality and we have said: No, that's too sharp, you can easily arrest someone, arrest him, you have to change that! – Now it’s like this: The judge must prove to the perpetrator two relevant acts within one year, and he must prove that he can and wishes to achieve with his deed 400 € per month of income. These points are so restrictive that we have immediately determined: This provision is pointless! It will not take hold and will lead to the fact that the perpetrators cannot be detained anymore, especially in the area of drugs. The second point that comes to this is that you have changed the addiction law so that when someone is attacked with the smallest amounts of drugs and he says that he needs it for his own needs, no longer automatically an ad takes place, but when he is cooperative, he can work with the health authorities. As a result, judges, prosecutors, but above all those police who have to do in the drug sector, have run into the door and have said: Please, this is a very bad arrangement, so that we will not be able to fight the problem, especially on the streets, but especially in the drugs sector at all! – And suddenly, at the beginning of this year, one has noticed the result. The drug scene – especially on the streets – has increased dramatically, as drug criminals are usually very well informed. They knew exactly: Well, from 1st. In fact, there is nothing more that can happen to us, for no one can prove us two acts! – We are also largely talking about criminal tourism. The criminal may not be in the country at all: He commits his act, then maybe he's gone a few months ago! At any rate, it was known in criminal tourism and, above all, the drug trade also knew: ==References== We're better than that. And immediately one noticed the result – it also went through all the media –: Along the U6, but also at other points in this city, but also, of course, in other cities, everywhere, in reality in all the agglomerations, one has immediately recognized how this has increased. Even if that was perhaps too far-reaching in the specific Judicature – i.e. by judges – this does not mean that the determination is not correct for a long time. You have to prove the intention of the perpetrator to secure an income by doing so. ParlaMint-AT_2016-04-28-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00126_d8e7590	Mr President. Minister! It is now about two things we are negotiating. One is the extension of court practice and the better remuneration of legal practitioners who complete the year of the court. Anyway, that makes sense. We have initially shortened the court practice to five months, now we will extend it to seven months. The occasion at that time was budgetary reasons which I could not quite understand, since the Ministry of Justice is the one with the highest degree of self-evidentness. I do not think it was useful at the time to save explicitly in the court year, because it is about young judges as candidates having the opportunity to obtain appropriate, high-quality training. The fact that it has been shortened has certainly brought with it a significant disadvantage. In that respect, it is good that we take that back again or at least extend the court practice. The other point concerns remuneration. This is also a point of comparison with the administrative practitioners. In any case, this makes sense, and it is also important that young people receive a corresponding remuneration at the time of the court year. The second point we are discussing here is the originally remembered merger of the district court Purkersdorf and the district court Hietzing as one, namely in Hietzing. I have already said in the Committee that I reserve my consent, and I am now a counter-voicer because we will not continue to agree. I am not concerned about whether the BG Purkersdorf and the BG Hietzing should be put together, but I am concerned primarily with the history behind it. It should perhaps be allowed to be broken on the tongue again, because it reflects very clearly how the government parties, on the one hand, on the one hand, and on the other hand, take useful measures which could be used for budgetary purposes by bringing together district courts and, on the other hand, dealing with the Austrian federal constitution. In the context of the administrative reform, it has been decided to put together appropriate district courts in Austria, because there was originally still a provision from the monarchy that within a day one must reach a district court with a horse car. Now one has noticed, about a hundred years later, that this is no longer contemporary and that, accordingly, one can put together without further district courts. This is a sensible measure, and that can save a lot of money. It is only then that we have forgotten that there is a law, a constitutional provision that provides for a so-called cut-off ban, by which district borders and courts are not allowed to cut. That's what you ignored at the time. The then Minister of Justice Karl is said to have received an opinion on the Constitutional Service. We asked at that time if there ever was an opinion. The Constitutional Service replied that there was none. That means that everyone could actually have seen that this is clearly unconstitutional, because this transitional provision from 1920 clearly provided that there is this “forbidden of snow” and because, accordingly, one cannot simply put together court appeals, at least they must not cut themselves with district boundaries. At that time, one should simply have sought a constitutional majority, and I believe that most of the Members of this House would have agreed to this, because of course it is not understandable why we still assume that we can be at the District Court with a horse car within one day. But you didn't do that, and you just did it. Then a judge from the Enns District Court, which was closed, went against it, brought it to the Constitutional Court and, of course, got right, as it was quite clear that one cannot do this. A constitutional majority has then been sought here in the house in order not to create a huge problem, insofar as all the decisions of the relevant courts would have probably been null and void. Of course, a two-thirds majority agreed willingly, since it makes sense to bring together district courts, and since they did so in the 21st century. As a result, it must be clear that there are not so many people in need of it. It is only the law and the corresponding constitutional provision that need to be adapted accordingly in advance. We have agreed, and I still think it makes sense to have district courts brought together. That is also one of the reasons why we will not agree to this withdrawal of the merger of the BG Purkersdorf and the BG Hietzing. In principle, it makes sense to put together district courts and if that goes well – and in that case it would be a useful solution. That is what you originally agreed on. Now you've been thinking: You don't want that anymore. That is, in that case, we would not have any savings, but it would cost as much as before. The tension is that the corresponding application states that one is taking this back because there are alleged constitutional concerns. At the beginning, I was extremely irritated because I thought that we had long solved the fundamental question, namely that of the ban on snow. There is no longer any “snowing ban” and, accordingly, a district court – in the case of Hietzing and some others – may also be responsible for several districts, even beyond the border with Lower Austria. The application then says somewhere that this transitional law – according to the spirit and the division – only regulates the relationship between the federal government and countries and not between the countries. It also says that it is not possible to create a corresponding competence with two different legal sources, namely, one regulation and one law, and, accordingly, there is no possibility of doing so. That is why there are major constitutional concerns, and we must take them back. It is now quite absurd that, as I told you before, when we all knew that there was the “forbidden of snow,” no one obviously thought that there were constitutional concerns. And now the reason is that there are so great constitutional concerns that you have to separate Purkersdorf and Hietzing again or not allow the merger. The Minister of Justice has said in the Committee that, in his opinion, there are no constitutional concerns. I share this assessment. I think it would have been possible without further delay, and I believe that it would have been useful to merge these two district courts because it also entails corresponding savings. It is rather true that there has been resistance to justice. The Minister of Justice himself has said that Purkersdorf is growing, and accordingly Purkersdorf has practically also, I do not say a right now, but reasons why a district court could be useful there and it would not have been the case at BG Hietzing. What would be important to me in this regard – and therefore we will not agree with this here – is that the request should include a statement of reasons which is also valid and, if one believes that these two District Courts should not be brought together, and does not put any schine arguments in the foreground, since, as so often in this House, it is a major problem as the government parties deal with the Austrian Constitution. In that case, they are not ignored, but they are drawn to find a schina argument for why these two district courts cannot be put together. I do not think this makes sense, and so we will not agree with it today.

A few years ago, in this House, hidden in the budget accompanying laws, it was decided quietly and secretly that the duration of the legal practice should be from nine to five months and at the same time reduced the training contribution by 20 percent. We have criticized this and warned against taking this step. This is precisely the reason why we need to increase from five to seven months today. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] We have warned about the deterioration of training, and we have stressed that serious and meaningful training with this reduction is simply no longer possible. It must also be said, however, that the so-called Year of Judgment is a truly valuable component in legal training for the emerging notaries, lawyers and all the legal professions involved. It's about getting to know the file run. That's what it's about knowing administrative processes. This is about gaining experience in dealing with people who are very often in crisis situations and who come to justice. This is about simply getting to know the various sectors of legal care in practice. All of this is part of it, and for that the five months were just too few. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] We therefore recognise your efforts, Mr Federal Minister, and will support this proposal. But please also allow me a personal remark: Please make sure that these seven months remain in fact seven months of training and that staff gaps are not so to speak, so to speak, that the legal practitioners are not suddenly in a writing-leader's activity and that the actual training can no longer take place. ParlaMint-AT_2016-04-28-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00126_d8e8370	We're on the 8th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Ing. — (DE) Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to speak on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party Big. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-04-28-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00126_d8e8397	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen. Dear listeners! We are now negotiating the implementation of an EU Directive on the collective exercise of copyright and related rights and the award of multi-territorial licences for musical works rights for online use in the internal market. The aim of this law, this EU directive, is to ensure transparency within collecting societies. This is done through a detailed billing for the individual rightholders and disclosure of the annual accounts and financial information, regardless of their legal form, which should also be examined accordingly. Transparency is the key word in this law, only in this way can the artists be sure that they will come to their right. I am pleased that we are still deciding today on an all-party proposal tabled by colleagues Zinggl, which calls for a more detailed breakdown of this information for the artists. We will be happy to agree. For the artists, it must be clear which rights they have, which tantimes they get, but it must also be clear to the users which fees they have to pay and that it does not result in a double fee, a double payment. In this respect, we have set out a negotiation procedure in this draft law. It is therefore necessary to call a common contact person during the negotiations with several collecting societies if this is required by the user associations; one speaks here of a central body. This is intended to allow user organisations, such as the specialised organisations in the Chamber of Commerce, to have an essential negotiating partner, since the collecting societies have to conduct the negotiations together. This, of course, has a very practical meaning when you imagine that, for example, many different copyrights can often be affected by the use of a work. Let's take as an example a song or a film. There are composers, textors, authors, performers, film producers and, if the film or song is broadcast on the radio, also broadcasting companies. And everyone I've mentioned now has rights to the movie or to the song, copyrights. These rights are exercised by different collecting societies, and for all collecting societies an agreement is necessary, i.e. an overall contract. In the government template, a single overall contract between user organisations and the different collecting societies was still mentioned. At the request of collecting societies, this has been changed to a majority of total contracts. This will allow each collecting society to conclude its own overall contract, but the negotiations will nevertheless be conducted jointly within the central body. This means that one can keep an overview, one cannot play one another out, because the negotiations must be conducted and ended together. This regulation has created an optimal compensation by allowing the user organisations to negotiate effectively, because there is this central body, on the other hand, as a final result for each collecting society, a separate contract in order to be able to carry out the distribution of their artists accordingly. This is a balanced result that gives both sides security. Negotiations can also be conducted in a manageable time frame. This gives security to both the user and the artist, and that is what this bill should bring. I would like to thank the negotiators for these often very lengthy negotiations, but they have led to a result-oriented solution with which I think all parties can live very well. - Thank you.

To grant third parties within the meaning of the Creative Commons. The latter is an opportunity for music-makers, authors or other art- and culture-makers to have a non-commercial use of their works, perhaps to increase their reputation or to serve artistically to the good purpose or to allow others to reinterpret or to further develop a work. I believe that Creative Commons licenses or similar licenses are also in the sense of the Sharing Economy. Similar to open-source software, free content and cost-based content are not in a competitive relationship with each other, but can add value to a symbiosis that leads to new business models, new products and new services. Finally, I also welcome the EU Commission's project on facilitating the award of multi-territorial licences for musical works. Last year, in the debate on copyright and storage media compensation, I mentioned that the future of music consumption lies in the streaming service. For streaming platforms providers in particular, however, there has so far been a major obstacle to offering the same works throughout Europe, because they have to apply for a licence per country. This also has an impact on the music creators in Austria, because it could thus be unattractive to offer the works of Austrian artists in another country, which affects the reach and awareness of our artists, whom, as I believe, we can also be very proud. All in all, I think this is a very good draft, which will also be approved here in the House and whose objective is absolutely supportive. That is why I would like to thank the Minister and his Ministry for the preparation of this draft and the two negotiators, the two SpÖ and ÖVP judges. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-04-28-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00126_d8e9020	We now come to points 9 and 10 of the agenda on which the debate is being held under one. There was no oral reporting. Mr Magistrate is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Stefan. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-04-28-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00126_d8e9047	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! This item on the agenda is about the Trade Representative Act, and it is a response to a judgment of the Supreme Court, namely that trade representatives – i.e. insurance representatives in terms of terms – enter into contracts and receive a commission for it. This commission can be either a one-off payment or in continuous payments, so-called follow-up commissions. There was now a dispute as to whether, if a contract between the insurance representative and the insurance company is terminated, the insurance representative will continue to receive his follow-up premiums. As I see it, the Supreme Court has stated: The performance of the insurance representative is provided, and it can therefore make no difference whether he receives his premium in a single payment or in a follow-up premium. In other words, these follow-up premiums will also be granted if the insurance contract is terminated. That is what the Supreme Court of Justice has found. So far, so comprehensible. Now, in order to undermine this judgment of the Supreme Court, a law is passed that in such a case at least 50 percent of the follow-up premiums must be paid in German 50 percent. Of course, in all the general terms and conditions of the insurance companies and in all contracts, it will be possible to pay 50 percent in such a case. That is, although the insurance representative has done his job and the Supreme Court has also attested it, the legislator now makes a provision, without our consent, that 50 percent less must be paid, i.e. the insurance representative receives 50 percent less for his performance. Apart from that, it is also questionable how any deduction payment is then calculated. This too, I believe, is completely open. It must also be remembered that such insurance representatives are practically only working with an insurance, i.e. are in a worker-like state, in other words in a dependency relationship. You know exactly when they quit: I'll lose 50 percent with this insurer. This is a very unpleasant situation, and these insurance representatives usually also have competition clauses, so they cannot work with another insurance. The insurance representatives are therefore completely blocked by this law and are further confirmed in their dependence; we believe that a system that is really not understandable. It is to be understood that, now that these follow-up premiums are now also legitimised by law, it is not possible to understand why the insurance industry in this case was offered the sugar: Yes, you can save 50 percent of the premiums in such a case. We are voting against this, and I hope that perhaps, thanks to my remarks, one or the other will come to the point of rethinking.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-04-28-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00126_d8e9097	Mr Vetter has been reported to speak. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-04-28-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00126_d8e9118	Mr President. Minister! High house! The market economy essentially belongs to freedom, including freedom of contract. Freedom of contract finds its limits where there is illegality or illegality. The beginning of this point, which we are discussing now, has been a judgment of the OGH, which has said that it is wrong when these follow-up commissions are completely eliminated, which must be said to be a very specific case, namely that of an insurance agent who was initially employed by the insurance undertaking, then concluded an agency contract with a ban on competition and cancelled after ten years. In this case, the Supreme Court of Justice has stated that a follow-up commission of 0 percent, the complete waste, is unsolicited. Before I go into it, by the way, I would like to draw a sentence from this judgment of the OGH, which stated: ‘In any event, apart from this, the legislative authority’s clear intention is to continue, so on and so on. If one reads such half-rates, one also reads out the wish of the Supreme Court for more stability of the legislator, that is, from us. I am simply handing this line of the Supreme Court over to us. On the matter itself: This § 26c is a so-called dispositive provision. These are special provisions for the insurance representatives in the Commercial Representatives Act. That is to say, this is a provision from which one can agree. That's what you decided to do. Now the Supreme Court is saying, no, so, in this one hundred percent kind, it is not possible. Now the representatives of the various parties are sitting together at the table, insurance companies and insurance agents. One has spoken with one another, and one has agreed that at least 50 percent of the commission should be granted. This was agreed in December. This is a compromise which does not, as the previous speaker said, oppose the recognition of the Supreme Court, but, quite the contrary, it implements it, because this realization says yes, it is not that zero states, but anything between zero and one hundred, and, as so often, one has landed at 50 percent. This is certainly a compromise – at least 50 percent – that is understandable, but it would not make sense to leave it in dispositive law. That is why we have added a second paragraph which makes it compulsory. That's logical, of course. I wonder if this would have been absolutely explicit. It would have to be self-evident, but in the sense of legal certainty it is certainly good if you write it in. There are still two aspects that are worthy of consideration, which we do not have in the legislative text, for which we have established a committee. One is that the follow-up commission depends on the premium payment. There's a lot of people who might say that's understood by themselves. However, it is expressly contained in § 26c, which is why we now also expressly write it in this provision or in the committee statement so that there can be no misunderstanding in this regard. The second is that this claim does not exist if the trader cancels or resolves the contractual relationship due to a debt, an important reason in accordance with § 22 Trade Representative's Act. It is also a matter of fairness – I believe, everyone – that in such a case one does not recognize the claim. Once again, I gather: It's an agreement. It is a minimum commission, and I believe that it is also a good solution in connection with this committee, which implements this recognition of the Supreme Court in an appropriate manner. - Thank you.

The materials are ultimately the implementation of an EU regulation. This EU Regulation concerns ‘specific requirements for the audit of public-interest undertakings’. These are banks, insurance companies and listed companies. It is not understandable why one does not negotiate these two legislative instruments in one, because it ultimately concerns matter that one improves the quality of the audit and, above all, ensures. In this case, we can say that one has been implemented into, one out. There was already an authority, namely the AeQ. These AeQ authorities, which are currently examining the auditors, are dissolved and transferred to the APAB. The big difference is that different qualifications of companies and auditors are presented in this new authority. On the one hand, we have these companies of public interest, which are to be examined. There is the full payment of this EU directive. We have new inspections, new and much more stringent procedures, as well as checks. The statutory audits required by Austrian federal law will be reviewed in this authority as before. Previously it was the AeQ. But I am particularly pleased that we have received your comments – those of the Greens, the Blues, the Blacks and the Reds – in order to take back a golden plating, which has already been given to AeQ, that we have redefined the scope of application that we have said: Is it necessary to do more than the EU tells us? We have decided that in the future small associations, small private foundations or even examinations that need to be carried out only under national law and not under federal law are not subject to this authority. What was the impact of this Golden Plating in the AeQ? – There are currently around 2,500 auditors in Austria, but this review could only be carried out 500. The market has been reduced by 80 percent, i.e. in principle less competition, tending to the Big Five – and there we have exactly the effects that Mrs Lichtenecker has described to us. Therefore: A risk-oriented approach is possible in this authority. It is not a golden plating, it is even a reconstruction, a deregulation of that authority accordingly provided. In addition, it was my particular concern to have transitional provisions, which are also practical. We have to decide this law today, because on 26 June we have to decide on it. In June, that authority shall start its operations. An authority is not built from today to tomorrow. ParlaMint-AT_2016-04-28-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00126_d8e10195	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! Values Advisors! The FPÖ will approve the now available version of the Auditor Supervisory Act, because various nonsenses still contained in the original draft law could still be eliminated by very constructive negotiations and discussions. One of these nonsensees was the composition of the Quality Audit Commission. The ministerial draft provided for seven more skilled members and seven skilled substitutes. With the government template, the number has doubled to 14 members and 14 replacement members. Why? – In the review procedure, the ÖGB has found that the occupation of this Commission has been criticised by the ÖGB for the exclusion of the social partners. I quote from the opinion of the ÖGB: "The Austrian Trade Union Confederation regrets the fact that the draft does not take advantage of the expertise of the social partners as regards the results of the quality assessment." I should like to say that the auditors are top-trained academics of a free profession, who have come out without the expertise of the social partners and will continue to do so in the future. It is not really useful to have the advisory function of a specialist body exercised by experts, as the ÖGB has proposed. This would be as if the social partners were without medical study about whether an operating room was properly equipped and if the right rules and rules for how an operation was carried out were complied with. [[Abg. Kogler: But this is the wrong picture!]] In the current legislation, the social partners are no longer represented in the Quality Audit Commission, and that is also good. The Auditor Supervisory Authority is a non-profit and independent federal authority, which the Federal Government wants to finance only with €500 000, i.e. around 20 percent of the total cost. The remaining 80 percent are in the profession and thus the economy. This level of funding is of constitutional concern because there is no financial independence from the Authority here. The Auditor Supervisory Authority is a Federal Authority and therefore the Federal Government should also participate with a higher share of funding. Today's motion for a resolution will ensure this. I would like to stress particularly positively that the amendment to the APAG's audit of associations and foundations and the mere revision of small cooperatives have been excluded from the supervision of the Auditor Supervisory Act. In addition, such mandatory audits are also excluded from supervision, which are only nationally arranged. As a result, the small auditors also have a better chance of survival. Today's motion for a resolution also ensures that, in the context of the next revision of the APAG, the absurd time-limitation system of certificates is replaced by a registration system, which provides for deletion from the register only if there are reasons for content, but not because of the time-limit of the certificate. I would therefore like to thank you for the following request. [[Abg. Rädler: Right time!]] [[Quoted print version removed]]

ParlaMint-AT_2016-04-28-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00126_d8e11322	My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The Auditor Supervisory Act is perhaps a law that really interests a few Austrians. However, the results of the functioning of this law are all very, very intense. If we had perhaps had had this law 20 years ago, and if it had already worked as it would then, then we would probably have saved ourselves from the BAWAG Committee, because it was the very problem that unfortunately, unfortunately, some numbers were very creative. That's the real background. Auditing means nothing but that the business outcome of a company must be presented. It is necessary to show the company’s profitability and the value of the company must be presented. This presentation is somehow clear to the ordinary citizen: If it's worth something, it's worth something if it's not worth anything, nothing stands there. Unfortunately, however, it is precisely assessment issues that are a science of their own, in which many can live out very much. Since this creativity of the final design then very often induces us to present values that are not at all present, balance sheets are often not very meaningful – the word “often” I want to delete again – some times were not very meaningful. This is really important to development in recent times. We have learned that representations of an economic result must be very accurate and sustainable – in all areas, whether it be an insurance, a bank, a savings bank or a cooperative. Companies of public interest, of real importance to all of us. It is therefore important that the audits of these companies work in such a way that the results hold a hundred percent. We know that wherever people are working, there are temptations, there are mistakes, there are habits, perhaps some practice that leads to unwanted results. In order for that not to be the case, it is necessary to check the one who checks. This quality assurance has existed since 2005 in a form that was not as bad, but also not as good as it should be. That is why we have now regulated with this Auditor Supervisory Act exactly how the quality assurance of the auditors must be done, how the experts have to evaluate how the relevant audits should be presented, so that they really work at the last stage, and how in all forms the results are really publicly evaluated. If then a examiner becomes sloppy, sluggish, or perhaps does not produce the results that his professional honor and knowledge should correspond to, then he is punished. That is the task of this authority. If this authority works well if quality assurance works well, then never one will be sanctioned because no one makes a mistake – and that is actually the goal of ensuring the quality of the balance sheets. Everyone should be able to rely that what is there is true, and that the one who works in such a business can then be sure that his job is good because those who run the management can build on good numbers and know how to make their decisions. In reality, this is a very important law, even if it is very few really interested. I hope it works and that many will vote in favour so that it can be decided well. - Thank you.

The centrepiece is the collection of tolls for transport-related air pollution and noise caused by road freight transport on the Austrian motorway and highway network. In addition, technical changes to the Federal Roads Act and the ASFINAG Act are also being made with this revision today. This is about the criminal situation in the use of special-vehicle routes without the proper payment of the fee – there is an administrative penalty – or also the adjustment of the calculation period for the annual valorisation of the tariff of the truck toll to that of the annual valorization of the vignette. It is thus a historic step today that external costs, such as air pollution or noise pollution, are included and taken into account. All in all, this innovation will make a significant contribution to the cost reliability and cost transparency of transport. This was, I think, also the demand of the Greens for a long time, or it is still. The additional revenue must be transferred to the federal government and will be used for the sustainable design of transport spaces. I should like to comment on two specific points in the debate. The one concerns the emission-related decoration. This does not go away, but will be done in future by the charge of external costs. The maximum demand will be redefined on the base network from 2017 – which is around 90 percent of the network. In any event, low-pollutant vehicles continue to be significantly favoured by tariffs. The Euro-VI truck will also receive an additional tariff bonus of €20 million on infrastructure charges by 2021. As a result, the Euro-VI truck is also located on the Brenner corridor of the A 12 and A 13, where additional charges on external costs are not allowed as a result of the cross-financing surcharges already added under the Road Costs Directive, in order to benefit from a lower tariff than the less environmentally friendly vehicles. Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that today's decision-making will implement an essential objective of Austrian transport policy, which is to implement economically sound and cost-transparent measures.

The cash settlement service has been shifted from switch to machine. The operation of a banknote also costs something, but these costs are often included in the cost of account management. However, I understand what I already understand is that client fees are lifted from such banks that themselves do not provide a banknote network or do not operate bank branches, but are only available on the Internet. As a fee to be required is very understandable to me because the structure that others receive would otherwise only be exploited and only benefits derived from it. However, when it comes to the cost of banknote settlements, a second point in this law is interesting, namely the better comparability of banking services such as banknote settlements. The aim of this provision is to make it transparent how much an account costs and, above all, what services are offered to enable comparison with other providers. In future, banks will have to publish the costs for the customer according to standardised criteria, and then it will be possible for a reasonable consumer to compare the costs for his bank account with the costs of another provider and find the best conditions for him. In other areas, this works very well, and I am also sure that this measure will strengthen consumers and help him save costs in the medium term by choosing the right offer. It also creates sufficient competition, which does not make it necessary to adopt a law which intervenes in the pricing of companies at a certain point. In order to clarify the matter with banknote fees, Minister of Finance Schelling will have a conversation with the banks on Monday – talk and not make a phone call. As regards the price/price increase application, I have to say that, as I believe, it does not correspond to the meaning and purpose of the Consumer Protection Act, it is under the cover of consumer protection pricing.

The main difference to a so-called normal payment account is that there is no overdrafting framework. That is, an overload is not possible, and therefore it is also suitable for people with debt. However, this account is equipped with bank card, online banking and with the possibility of carrying out permanent orders and direct debits. The request is clearly too late, because at present the Social Ministry is under an in-depth examination of whether there will be legal possibilities to prevent such a fee, but which must also be covered by European and constitutional law. As I said, The work is already underway in the Ministry of Social Affairs. Perhaps still on the current legal situation in this area without such a ban: At present, the situation is such that a banknote fee can be introduced in the case of new contracts. However, the proposals that are already being used in the media are illegal in many areas, I think, and I am convinced that consumer protection organisations will keep an intensive eye on this in the future. The current old contracts are a little more critical. As a result, the introduction of a new fee in the case of bank card settlements is only permitted by the express consent of the consumer and thus, ultimately, by means of a notice of amendment. As has already been said, there is already intensive work in the field of the Social Ministry. In the area of the basic account, the benchmarks have already been reached many times. 30 million people within the European Union are affected, 150 000 people – I really want to talk about people – are also affected in Austria. We're talking about homeless people, we're talking about people without income, we're talking about people who're overdebted. All these people so far have a considerable disadvantage than they have to make cash deposits for their daily lives, and these cash deposits are now more expensive than cashless payments. In other words, it is only socially weak. the people and people under financial pressure are also subject to higher charges. By this law now exists at the end of the 18th day. It is precisely for these stigmatised people in Austria, who have a legitimate residence and permanent residence in the EU, who are entitled to a basic account. I do not wish to mention that these people have so far been disadvantaged in the area of the labour market and job search, as employers in many areas have no longer planned cash disbursement. The account will also have all the necessary functions of a normal payment account. The limits of €40 and €80 will be set by the Ministry of Social Affairs for the eligible groups in the summer. Summa summarum I would like to say that this basic account is an important and appropriate measure in the field of consumer protection. With the implementation of this EU directive, and also with regard to the obstacles and disadvantages raised, I am very much in favour of this law and welcome it. Thank you.
